Hello, Doctor. Thank you for seeing me. I, um... I'm sorry to take up your time. I know you're very busy. I'm just here because... well, my supervisor at work actually suggested I come in. But lately, I've just been having these... these passing thoughts. Nothing crazy, I promise. Just... sometimes I think maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up tomorrow. Like maybe my family wouldn't have to worry about me so much. It's silly, really, just stress talking, I'm sure. But I mentioned it at work, and they got a little worried, so I figured I should come in, just to be safe. Is... is that okay? I hope I'm not overreacting by being here.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor, I guess... I mean, I don't want to sound like a complainer. I really do have a good life. But, um, to answer your question... yeah, maybe a little bit. 

I used to always keep pretty busy. I'd mess with my tomato plants out back, or go out and sand wood at my bench. I always liked watching the Falcons, too, and... well, I used to be the guy who'd stay after church just talking with everybody for a good while. But lately... I don't know. I just look at all that like it belongs to somebody else.

Well... I mean, I try not to think like that. I know I've got a lot to be thankful for, and I don't want to sit here and throw a pity party. I always try to do my best by everyone, you know? 

But... since you're asking... I guess I have been having a hard time with some things lately. It's just... my daughter moved out a while back, and... I don't know, I just keep going over it in my head. I keep telling myself I must have failed her somehow. Like... if I had just been a better father, maybe I would have done something different, and things wouldn't be the way they are. 

I know it probably sounds silly, and I really shouldn't be dwelling on it. I'm sure it's just normal parent stuff, right? Everyone worries they didn't do a good enough job. I just... I hope I'm not making too much out of it. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor, I... I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I really don't want you to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself all day. I know I'm a grown man and I should have a better handle on things. 

But, um... to be completely honest with you... yeah, I guess I do blame myself a lot. It always comes back to my daughter moving out. I just... I can't seem to stop going over it in my mind, replaying everything. I keep telling myself that I must have failed her somehow. You know, like... if I had just been a better father, if I had done things differently, maybe she wouldn't have needed to leave the way she did. 

I know it's probably not right to think that way, and I'm sure you hear this kind of thing all the time. It's just hard to shake that feeling of being a failure, even if other folks might not see it that way. I'm trying to work on it, I really am. Am I answering your questions okay, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... Doctor, I feel like I'm just making a mess of this whole interview. I'm so sorry if I'm not giving you the answers you need. I really, truly want to be cooperative. 

But, um... no, honestly, before this past year, there was never anything that got in the way of my life like that. I've always been proud of being dependable, you know? Showing up for work, being there for my family.
